independent travel?\n\nBudget tours can be worthwhile when transportation and guiding are shared efficiently.\n\nIndependent travel can be cheaper if you use modest hotels and local transportation, but it requires more planning.\n\nConsider an attraction that takes several separate taxi journeys to visit independently. A shared excursion may become financially competitive when the transportation and guide are included.\n\nThe best comparison is total cost, time, and convenience.\n\nTravelers watching their expenses can evaluate [budget tours in Egypt](https://egypttimetravel.com/budget-tours-in-egypt/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) alongside a self-organized itinerary.\n\n## Are All-Inclusive Egypt Holidays Worth the Money?\n\nAn all-inclusive Egypt holiday can provide predictable spending when you expect to use the services included in the package.\n\nHowever, the definition of all-inclusive varies between hotels and tour operators.\n\nA Red Sea resort holiday may include accommodation, buffet meals, selected drinks, and some hotel entertainment.\n\nA multi-city cultural package might include accommodation, sightseeing, transfers, domestic transportation, and guides—but not every meal or optional experience.\n\nThese are two different products.\n\n### When is all-inclusive a good choice?\n\nAn inclusive booking can suit travelers who:\n\n- Want fewer separate payments.\n\n- Prefer a resort-based holiday.\n\n- Are traveling with children.\n\n- Want transfers and activities arranged in advance.\n\n- Prefer a clearer initial vacation budget.\n\n\n### When might an all-inclusive booking be poor value?\n\nIt may offer less value when you expect to spend most days outside the hotel.\n\nFor example, travelers planning multiple full-day excursions may not use the included resort lunches, daytime activities, or amenities.\n\nAn all-inclusive resort package can still be convenient, but you should compare it against a bed-and-breakfast booking plus the activities you genuinely intend to purchase.\n\nRead the exact inclusions when comparing [all-inclusive Egypt holidays](https://egypttimetravel.com/egypt-holidays-all-inclusive/?utm_source=chatgpt.com).\n\n## What Visa and Entry Costs Should You Budget for Egypt?\n\nVisa requirements depend on nationality, travel purpose, entry circumstances, and current Egyptian government regulations.\n\nAt the time of research, Egypt’s official e-Visa information lists a tourist single-entry visa fee of US$30 and a multiple-entry tourist e-Visa fee of US$65 for eligible applicants. Requirements and eligibility should be checked again before travel.\n\nNot every traveler follows the same visa procedure.\n\nCertain nationalities may have different rules, and separate conditions can apply to specific entry categories.\n\nTravelers should verify their personal circumstances rather than assuming that an online visa or visa on arrival is available to everyone.\n\n### How far in advance should you arrange your visa?\n\nEgypt’s official electronic visa portal advises submitting an application at least seven days before departure.\n\nHowever, travelers should allow additional time for unexpected complications and document checks.\n\nFor a 2027 visit, confirm requirements before committing to non-refundable travel arrangements.\n\nThe [Egypt entry requirements guide](https://egypttimetravel.com/entry-to-egypt/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) provides useful background for planning.\n\n### What other documents may create travel expenses?\n\nDepending on your nationality and circumstances, the cost of preparing for travel may involve:\n\n- Passport renewal.\n\n- Visa processing.\n\n- Travel insurance.\n\n- Necessary supporting documents.\n\n- Required personal travel preparations.\n\n\nTravel insurance should not be treated as an optional detail merely because it is inexpensive relative to international airfare.\n\nMedical treatment, trip disruption, lost baggage, and other unexpected problems can create substantial costs.\n\nChoose appropriate coverage for the actual activities you intend to undertake.\n\n## How Can You Reduce Your Egypt Trip Cost Without Missing the Best Experiences?\n\nSaving money does not have to mean reducing the vacation to the cheapest possible services.\n\nThe most effective savings usually come from improving the itinerary rather than compromising every individual purchase.\n\n### Spend more nights in fewer cities\n\nEvery hotel change introduces possible transfers, luggage handling, travel time, and logistical complications.\n\nFor a seven-day holiday, consider concentrating on two regions.\n\nFor a 10-day vacation, choose a manageable route rather than adding stops simply because they are famous.\n\nThe extra time gained can improve sightseeing and reduce transport expenses.\n\n### Choose your most important paid attractions\n\nNot every temple, tomb, and museum needs to be included in one visit.\n\nFor example, a first-time traveler might prioritize the Giza Plateau, the Grand Egyptian Museum, Karnak Temple, and the Valley of the Kings.\n\nA specialist interested in royal tombs may prefer additional time in Luxor rather than expensive activities elsewhere.\n\nThe correct choice depends on your interests.\n\n### Combine nearby attractions\n\nGeographic planning can reduce unnecessary travel.\n\nIn Cairo, group attractions sensibly according to location and road conditions.\n\nIn Luxor, it may be practical to organize East Bank and West Bank visits separately.\n\nIn Aswan, plan river-dependent activities around the available boat arrangements and your wider itinerary.\n\nA geographically efficient itinerary can save both money and energy.\n\n### Use guides where they add the most value\n\nA knowledgeable Egyptologist can make major archaeological sites more understandable.\n\nHowever, you may not need a private guide for every activity.\n\nPrioritize guiding at complex historical sites, then enjoy selected markets, restaurants, and leisure areas independently when appropriate.\n\n### Compare tour inclusions before choosing a price\n\nA tour advertising an attractive price can become significantly more expensive if it excludes admission tickets, airport transfers, domestic flights, and meals.\n\nCreate a simple comparison sheet and place the final price of equivalent services side by side.\n\nA quote that costs more initially can be the better financial decision if it includes the expenses you would otherwise purchase separately.\n\n### Give yourself an emergency reserve\n\nDo not spend your entire available budget before departure.\n\nA practical planning approach is to keep an additional reserve, perhaps around 10–15% of your estimated controllable trip expenses, where financially possible.\n\nThat percentage is a recommendation, not a compulsory travel charge.\n\nThe reserve can help absorb unexpected transportation costs, exchange-rate movements, or itinerary changes.\n\n## Common Egypt Travel Budget Mistakes\n\n### Mistake: Comparing package prices without checking what is included\n\nA package with domestic flights, tickets, guides, and transfers is not directly comparable with a hotel-only arrangement.\n\nAlways calculate the complete service cost.\n\n### Mistake: Assuming every pyramid ticket includes interior access\n\nCertain pyramid interiors have separate admission requirements.\n\nDecide which experiences matter before purchasing tickets.\n\n### Mistake: Forgetting the cost of traveling between cities\n\nCairo, Luxor, and Aswan are not adjacent neighborhoods.\n\nMoving between them can require flights, trains, road journeys, and additional transfers.\n\n### Mistake: Treating an all-inclusive hotel as an all-inclusive vacation\n\nResort packages may exclude excursions, airport transfers, premium dining, or activities outside the property.\n\nRead the booking conditions.\n\n### Mistake: Using an outdated Egyptian pound exchange rate\n\nExchange-rate movements can change the converted cost of hotels, meals, tickets, and other services.\n\nUse current conversion information when paying.\n\n### Mistake: Budgeting for every famous attraction\n\nAn excessive sightseeing schedule increases admission expenses while leaving less time to enjoy each place.\n\nChoose attractions by interest, geography, and practical duration.\n\n### Mistake: Forgetting tips and small expenses\n\nWater, snacks, laundry, discretionary gratuities, and local transport can add up during a longer trip.\n\nAllow room for ordinary personal spending.\n\n### Mistake: Choosing travel dates only because flights are cheap\n\nA low airfare during a very hot period may not represent the best overall value if you plan extensive outdoor sightseeing in Upper Egypt.\n\nWeather and comfort matter.\n\n### Mistake: Booking the cheapest hotel without considering location\n\nA low nightly rate can lead to more spending on taxis or private transport.\n\nEvaluate accommodation and transportation together.\n\n### Mistake: Not checking cancellation policies\n\nTravel plans can change.\n\nBefore paying, review refund eligibility, date-change charges, payment deadlines, and what happens when a booked service is altered.\n\n## How to Build Your Own Egypt Travel Budget for 2027\n\nThe most reliable method is to prepare a budget using real booking prices for fixed expenses and reasonable allowances for spending that remains flexible.\n\nStart with the destination route, not the hotel.\n\n### Step 1: Choose the number of days\n\nDecide whether you have seven, 10, or 14 days, including your arrival and departure days.\n\nDo not count a late-night arrival as a full sightseeing day.\n\n### Step 2: Select the main places you want to visit\n\nPrioritize the regions that matter to you.\n\nA classic first-time itinerary might include Cairo and Luxor, with Aswan added for a longer stay.\n\nIf beaches are more important, choose an itinerary with additional time along the Red Sea.\n\n### Step 3: Price international flights\n\nCompare real fares for the exact dates, including baggage and connection costs.\n\nConsider multi-city flights when useful.\n\n### Step 4: Calculate accommodation per room\n\nUse the actual nightly hotel rate and multiply it by the number of nights.\n\nAccount for room occupancy, breakfast, taxes, and resort charges where applicable.\n\nDivide shared expenses appropriately when calculating per-person costs.\n\n### Step 5: Add transportation between cities\n\nEstimate domestic flights, trains, cruise movements, or private transfers.\n\nDo not use a local taxi allowance to cover long-distance travel.\n\n### Step 6: Add sightseeing\n\nList your priority attractions and current admission prices.\n\nInclude separate tickets for special experiences.\n\n### Step 7: Add meals and personal spending\n\nChoose a realistic daily food allowance.\n\nAdd tips and discretionary purchases where applicable.\n\n### Step 8: Include entry and protection costs\n\nAccount for visa requirements, travel insurance, and other relevant preparations.\n\n### Step 9: Add a contingency amount\n\nKeep an additional reserve for price changes and unexpected spending.\n\n### Step 10: Compare independent and guided arrangements\n\nOnce you know what the independent itinerary would cost, compare it with a complete guided package offering equivalent services.\n\nThe difference may be smaller—or larger—than the headline prices suggest.\n\nTravelers who want help organizing the route can use the [Egypt trip planner](https://egypttimetravel.com/egypt-trip-planner/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) as the next step.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions About Trip to Egypt Costs\n\n### Can you visit Egypt for $1,000?\n\nYes, a short Egypt vacation can fit within a $1,000 ground budget when travelers select modest accommodation and manage sightseeing and transportation carefully. However, international airfare can make the complete trip considerably more expensive. A traveler with $1,000 for everything, including a long-haul return flight, should check flight prices before assuming the trip is affordable.\n\n### How much does a five-day trip to Egypt cost?\n\nUsing the provisional 2027 daily allowances, five days of basic ground spending could require around $225–$375 on a budget or $500–$925 at mid-range. These estimates exclude international flights and major additional expenses. Short trips can have a comparatively high daily total because airport transfers, visas, and intercity travel are spread across fewer days.\n\n### Are attraction tickets cheaper for students?\n\nSome Egyptian archaeological sites and museums publish discounted student categories. Eligibility can depend on age, nationality, valid documentation, and the attraction’s specific rules. Travelers should not assume that any student card automatically qualifies. Check the current ticket conditions for each attraction before including discounted rates in the budget.\n\n### Can tourists pay by credit card in Egypt?\n\nPayment cards are accepted at many hotels, restaurants, and organized tourist services, but visitors should not assume every business accepts cards. Keeping a reasonable amount of Egyptian pounds is useful for small purchases and situations where electronic payment is unavailable. Check foreign transaction fees and currency conversion conditions with your card issuer.\n\n### Do children pay the same prices as adults in Egypt?\n\nNot always. Some museums and attractions have child admission categories or free-entry rules for qualifying young children. Hotels, airlines, and cruise operators may apply their own child-pricing and occupancy policies. Families should calculate the total using each child’s age and the specific service conditions rather than applying a universal discount.\n\n### Is booking a trip to Egypt early always cheaper?\n\nNo. Booking early can improve availability and may help secure preferred hotels, flights, and cruises, but prices do not always move in one direction. Promotions, seasonal demand, exchange rates, and cancellation policies all influence the final cost. Travelers should compare several suitable dates and avoid assuming that a lower non-refundable rate is automatically the better deal.\n\n### Does an Egypt tour package usually include entrance fees?\n\nThere is no universal rule. Some tour packages include archaeological admission fees, while others list them as separate costs. Premium tombs, pyramid interiors, special exhibitions, and optional experiences may also require additional payment. Always obtain a written inclusion list before comparing packages.\n\n### How much extra should I budget for souvenirs in Egypt?\n\nSouvenir spending is entirely discretionary. A traveler purchasing only small gifts might set aside a modest amount, while someone shopping for jewelry, textiles, artwork, or larger decorative items may spend considerably more. Decide on a shopping allowance before visiting markets so that purchases do not reduce money reserved for transportation and essential expenses.\n\n### Do Egyptian hotels charge in US dollars or Egyptian pounds?\n\nPayment currency can vary by hotel, booking platform, and reservation terms. Some international booking systems display prices in US dollars, euros, or British pounds, while local payments may be processed in Egyptian pounds. Check the actual payment currency, conversion rules, taxes, and any additional fees before booking.\n\n### Will Egypt travel prices increase in 2027?\n\nSome prices may increase, decrease, or remain unchanged. Exchange rates, accommodation demand, government admission policies, airline fares, and operating costs can all influence travel expenses. A 2027 planning guide should therefore distinguish confirmed charges from estimated budgets and refresh prices as new official information becomes available.\n\n### How can I check whether my Egypt vacation budget is realistic?\n\nStart with actual airfare and accommodation quotations, then add intercity transportation, visas, major attraction admissions, meals, tips, and personal spending. Include a contingency reserve and compare the total with a similarly equipped guided package. This gives a more useful estimate than relying on a single national average for daily travel spending.\n\n## Final Thoughts: What Should You Really Budget for Egypt in 2027?\n\nThe most useful way to estimate your trip to Egypt cost in 2027 is to decide what you want to experience before choosing how much to spend.\n\nA budget vacation can provide excellent experiences through careful route planning, modest accommodation, and local dining.\n\nA mid-range trip offers greater comfort and can incorporate guided historical visits and efficient transportation.\n\nPremium travel provides more flexibility, upgraded accommodation, and personalized services, but it is not essential for appreciating Egypt’s history and culture.\n\nThe real financial differences usually come from where you stay, how often you move between cities, which attractions you visit, and how much assistance you want with travel arrangements.\n\nFor most first-time visitors, a well-paced itinerary covering Cairo and Upper Egypt is a stronger starting point than trying to visit every major attraction in one journey.\n\nIf you prefer a tailored approach, Egypt Time Travel can help compare accommodation standards, guided sightseeing, transportation, and Nile cruise options within your preferred budget.\n\n[Request a customized Egypt travel quotation](https://egypttimetravel.com/contact-us/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) with your intended dates, number of travelers, and preferred experiences so that the final price reflects the trip you actually want.\n\n[Prev](https://egypttimetravel.com/egypt-outdoor-activities/)[Next](https://egypttimetravel.com/eye-of-ra-vs-eye-of-horus/)\n\n[WhatsApp Chat Button](https://api.whatsapp.com/send?phone=201028002828&text)\n\n❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄❄\n\n🎅\n\n✦ FESTIVE SPECIAL ✦\n\n\n🎄 Christmas in Egypt 2027\n\nMagical Holiday Offers, Nile Cruises & Festive Tours\n\n\n[Explore Christmas Tours ↗](https://egypttimetravel.com/egypt-christmas-tours-and-packages/)",

It is home to more than 9.8 million people. The city is also part of the largest urban agglomeration in Africa, the Arab world, and the Middle East. The Greater Cairo metropolitan area is one of the largest in the world by population with over 22 million people. Compare fares into each one, as a slightly longer transfer can sometimes mean a much cheaper flight.\n\n- CAI**Cairo International Airport** 11 mi (19 km) from the city centre\n- CCE**Capital International Airport** 36 mi (59 km) from the city centre\n\n## Getting around Cairo\n### By metro\nCairo is home to Africa's first and largest metro system. While modern and sleek, the three lines are all too limited in scope. As of October 2024, a single trip ticket ranges from LE8-20, depending on the number of stations covered:\n\n### By bus\nThe large red, white and blue public buses cover the entire city and are much cheaper, costing LE4-6, but are usually crowded and slow. However, there are the similar air-conditioned buses that charge LE7-12. They can be found in the main squares in Cairo. Also found in main squares are the smaller mini-buses that are usually orange and white or red, white and blue. Because of problems with sexual harassment women travellers are advised only to take the small minibuses and buses which prohibit standing.\n\n### By taxi\nTaxis in Cairo are solid white. They are equipped with meters but these are not always used. They can be hailed from the street, and are common enough to be used perhaps exclusively (given a little patience) by any traveler.",

It is home to more than 9.8 million people. The city is also part of the largest urban agglomeration in Africa, the Arab world, and the Middle East. The Greater Cairo metropolitan area is one of the largest in the world by population with over 22 million people. Areas of what would become Cairo were inhabited from pre-dynastic and early-dynastic ancient Egypt c. 6000 years ago, as the Giza pyramid complex and the ancient cities of Memphis and Heliopolis are today within the city.\n\n## Cheap flights to Cairo\n\nThe lowest round-trip fare found by Destina Holidays travellers in the last 30 days was **$970** from New York, travelling 24 Nov – 1 Dec. These are the cheapest fares from the most searched cities.\n\n- [NYC**New York to Cairo** 24 Nov – 1 Dec · CAIfrom$970](https://www.destinaholidays.com/compare-flights/?from=NYC%2CCAI&to=CAI%2CNYC&depart=2026-11-24%2C2026-12-01&adult=1&child=0&infant=0&cabin=economy&flexible=false)\n\n### Cheapest month to fly\n\n**$970** Nov**$1,396** Dec\n\nAirlines travellers compared most often: ITA Airways, United Airlines.\n\nPrices are per adult in US dollars, taken from searches between 26 Sep and 26 Oct 2026. Fares change often, so search your dates for live prices.\n\n[Search flights to Cairo](https://www.destinaholidays.com/#flights)\n\n## Places to visit in Cairo\n\n1. ![Giza Pyramid Complex](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/96/Pyramids_of_the_Giza_Necropolis.jpg/960px-Pyramids_of_the_Giza_Necropolis.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Giza Pyramid Complex\n\nHistoric · 8 mi from centre\n\nThe Great Pyramid, its companion pyramids and the Great Sphinx stand on the desert plateau west of Cairo, among the most famous ancient monuments on Earth; arrive early to avoid heat.\n\n2. ![Grand Egyptian Museum](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Logo_of_the_Grand_Egyptian_Museum.svg/960px-Logo_of_the_Grand_Egyptian_Museum.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Grand Egyptian Museum\n\nMuseum · 8 mi from centre\n\nA vast museum near the Giza pyramids dedicated to ancient Egyptian civilization, including treasures from the tomb of Tutankhamun and a monumental statue of Ramesses II in its atrium.\n\n3. ![Egyptian Museum](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/1897_bis_1902_wurde_das_%C3%84gyptische_Museum_in_Kairo_gebaut._04.jpg/960px-1897_bis_1902_wurde_das_%C3%84gyptische_Museum_in_Kairo_gebaut._04.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Egyptian Museum\n\nMuseum\n\nThe historic museum on Tahrir Square holds a huge collection of pharaonic antiquities, including statues, sarcophagi and artifacts from many dynasties, in atmospheric old galleries.\n\n4. ![Khan el-Khalili](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/%D8%AE%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%D9%8A_1.jpg/960px-%D8%AE%D8%A7%D9%86_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AE%D9%84%D9%8A%D9%84%D9%8A_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Khan el-Khalili\n\nMarket · 2 mi from centre\n\nA historic bazaar in Islamic Cairo with narrow alleys of shops selling lamps, spices, textiles and souvenirs, plus traditional coffeehouses such as El Fishawy; bargaining is expected.\n\n5. ![Cairo Citadel](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Flickr_-_HuTect_ShOts_-_Citadel_of_Salah_El.Din_and_Masjid_Muhammad_Ali_%D9%82%D9%84%D8%B9%D8%A9_%D8%B5%D9%84%D8%A7%D8%AD_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%AF%D9%8A%D9%86_%D8%A7%D9%84%D8%A3%D9%8A%D9%88%D8%A8%D9%8A_%D9%88%D9%85%D8%B3%D8%AC%D8%AF_%D9%85%D8%AD%D9%85%D8%AF_%D8%B9%D9%84%D9%8A_-_Cairo_-_Egypt_-_17_04_2010_%284%29.jpg/960px-thumbnail.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Cairo Citadel\n\nHistoric · 2 mi from centre\n\nA medieval hilltop fortress built by Saladin, with the Ottoman-style Mosque of Muhammad Ali, several museums and terraces offering views across Cairo's skyline of minarets.\n\n6. ![Al-Azhar Mosque](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Mosqu%C3%A9e_Al-Azhar.jpg/960px-Mosqu%C3%A9e_Al-Azhar.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Al-Azhar Mosque\n\nReligious · 2 mi from centre\n\nOne of the oldest mosques in Cairo and a renowned center of Islamic learning, with a beautiful marble courtyard and minarets from various eras; visitors should dress modestly and remove shoes.\n\n7. ![Coptic Cairo](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Kairo_Hanging_Church_BW_1.jpg/960px-Kairo_Hanging_Church_BW_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Coptic Cairo\n\nReligious · 3 mi from centre\n\nAn old quarter with historic churches, including the Hanging Church, along with the Coptic Museum and Ben Ezra Synagogue, reflecting the city's long religious diversity.\n\n8. ![Saqqara](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2e/Saqqara_pyramid_ver_2.jpg/960px-Saqqara_pyramid_ver_2.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=api&utm_content=thumbnail)\n\n\n### Saqqara\n\nHistoric · 12 mi from centre\n\nSouth of Cairo, this vast necropolis is home to the Step Pyramid of Djoser, among the oldest large stone monuments, and many tombs with finely carved and painted walls.\n\n\n## Top experiences in Cairo\n\nTours, tickets and experiences you can book with Destina Holidays, from 79 available.\n\n[![Visit the Grand Egyptian Museum and the Pyramids - Compare fares into each one, as a slightly longer transfer can sometimes mean a much cheaper flight.\n\n- CAI**Cairo International Airport** 11 mi (19 km) from the city centre\n- CCE**Capital International Airport** 36 mi (59 km) from the city centre\n\n[Search flights to Cairo](https://www.destinaholidays.com/#flights)\n\n## Getting around Cairo\n\nYou will find that it's useful to have several maps handy if you are looking to get around Cairo on your own. Spellings of street and place names can vary from map to map and from map to actual location, and not every street will appear on every map.\n\n### By metro\n\nCairo is home to Africa's first and largest metro system. While modern and sleek, the three lines are all too limited in scope. As of October 2024, a single trip ticket ranges from LE8-20, depending on the number of stations covered:\n\n### By bus\n\nThe large red, white and blue public buses cover the entire city and are much cheaper, costing LE4-6, but are usually crowded and slow. However, there are the similar air-conditioned buses that charge LE7-12. They can be found in the main squares in Cairo. Also found in main squares are the smaller mini-buses that are usually orange and white or red, white and blue. Because of problems with sexual harassment women travellers are advised only to take the small minibuses and buses which prohibit standing.\n\n### By taxi\n\nTaxis in Cairo are solid white. They are equipped with meters but these are not always used. They can be hailed from the street, and are common enough to be used perhaps exclusively (given a little patience) by any traveler.\n\n## Cairo travel FAQ\n\nWhen is the best time to visit Cairo?\n\nBased on recent weather records, the most comfortable time to visit Cairo is November to March, when average daytime highs are around 70–81°F and rain falls on about 1 days a month.\n\nWhat is the weather like in Cairo?\n\nJuly is usually the warmest month in Cairo, with average highs of 104°F (40°C). February is the coolest, with average lows of 50°F (10°C). The wettest month is typically January, with about 3 mm of rain.\n\nWhich airport is closest to Cairo?\n\nCairo International Airport (CAI) is the closest airport, about 11 miles (19 km) from the centre of Cairo. Other nearby airports include Capital International Airport (CCE, 36 mi).\n\nHow much are flights to Cairo?\n\nThe cheapest round-trip fare Destina Holidays travellers found to Cairo in the last 30 days was $970 from New York, travelling 24 Nov – 1 Dec. November is currently the cheapest month to fly, with fares from $970.\n\nWhat currency is used in Cairo?\n\nCairo uses the Egyptian pound (EGP). At the latest exchange rate, 1 US dollar ≈ £55.7.\n\nWhat time zone is Cairo in?\n\nCairo uses the Africa/Cairo time zone (currently UTC+3). Remember to check for daylight saving changes when planning connecting flights.\n\nHow do I get around Cairo?\n\nYou will find that it's useful to have several maps handy if you are looking to get around Cairo on your own. Spellings of street and place names can vary from map to map and from map to actual location, and not every street will appear on every map.\n\nWhat are the best places to visit in Cairo?\n\nTop sights in Cairo include Giza Pyramid Complex, Grand Egyptian Museum, Egyptian Museum and Khan el-Khalili. The Great Pyramid, its companion pyramids and the Great Sphinx stand on the desert plateau west of Cairo, among the most famous ancient monuments on Earth; arrive early to avoid heat.\n\nWhat are the top things to do in Cairo?\n\nPopular experiences you can book in Cairo include Visit the Grand Egyptian Museum and the Pyramids - Full-Day Tour, Nile Dinner Cruise and Pyramids and Museum (Excluding Grand Egyptian Museum) - Private Full-Day. Destina Holidays offers 79 tours and activities here.\n\nHow many hotels can I book in Cairo?\n\nDestina Holidays currently lists 588 properties in and around Cairo, from budget stays to luxury hotels.         "url": "https://elsahabytours.com/cairo-travel-guide/",
        "title": "Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Things to Do, Where to Stay & Costs",
        "description": "5 days ago · Use this Cairo travel guide 2026 to plan the Pyramids, GEM, Historic Cairo, hotels, transport, costs, safety and the best things to do.",
        "position": 4,
        "markdown": "DRAG\n\n- [Home](https://elsahabytours.com/)\n- [Egypt Travel Guide](https://elsahabytours.com/category/egypt-travel-guide/)\n- [Destination Guides](https://elsahabytours.com/category/egypt-travel-guide/destination-guides/)\n- Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Best Things to Do, Where to Stay, Costs & Travel Tips\n\n![Best things to do in Cairo in 2026 including the Giza Pyramids, museums, mosques, markets and Nile experiences](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-17.png)\n\n[Info@elsahabytours.com](https://elsahabytours.com/author/admin/) [October 20, 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/2026/10/20/) [![](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/themes/tourm/assets/img/icon/map.svg)Destination Guides](https://elsahabytours.com/category/egypt-travel-guide/destination-guides/)\n\nA good **Cairo travel guide 2026** needs to explain much more than how to see the Pyramids.\n\nCairo is one of the most layered cities in the world. In the same trip, you can stand beside the last surviving Wonder of the Ancient World, explore royal treasures inside the Grand Egyptian Museum, walk through medieval streets, visit centuries-old mosques and churches, drink tea in a historic bazaar, cruise the Nile at night and experience the energy of a modern capital that never seems to slow down.\n\nFor many travelers, Cairo is the first introduction to Egypt. It is also one of the easiest places to misunderstand.\n\nTraffic can be intense. Distances are larger than they appear on a map. Giza and central Cairo are not the same area. Some attractions deserve half a day while others can be combined efficiently. Choosing the wrong hotel location can add hours to your trip, while a well-planned itinerary can make Cairo one of the most rewarding parts of Egypt.\n\nThis complete Cairo travel guide explains how to plan your visit in 2026, including the best attractions, how many days you need, where to stay, transportation, entrance costs, suggested itineraries, food, safety, common mistakes and local planning advice.\n\nFor current private tours and experiences, see the Elsahaby Tours Cairo destination page. [Explore Cairo tours and experiences](https://elsahabytours.com/destinations/cairo/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n![Cairo at a glance with pyramids, Nile, skyline, attractions, transport, best areas to stay and travel planning tips](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-30-1024x576.png)A quick overview of Cairo covering the best time to visit, ideal stay, major attractions, neighborhoods, transportation, airport and travel pace.\n\n## Cairo at a Glance\n\n| Travel Question | Quick Answer |\n| --- | --- |\n| Best time to visit Cairo | October to April for more comfortable outdoor sightseeing |\n| Ideal stay | 3–4 full sightseeing days |\n| Minimum recommended stay | 2 full days |\n| Main attractions | Giza Pyramids, Grand Egyptian Museum, Historic Cairo, Old Cairo, NMEC |\n| Best area for first-time visitors | Depends on itinerary: Giza, Zamalek, Downtown or Garden City |\n| Main airport | Cairo International Airport |\n| Best transportation for sightseeing | Private car or trusted ride-hailing service |\n| Metro useful? | Yes for selected central routes, but not for every major attraction |\n| Best day trip | Saqqara, Memphis & Dahshur |\n| Best evening experience | Al-Muizz Street, Khan El Khalili or a Nile evening |\n| Currency | Egyptian Pound |\n| Language | Arabic; English is common in tourism |\n| Recommended pace | Do not combine too many distant attractions in one day |\n\n* * *\n\n## Why Visit Cairo in 2026?\n\nThis Cairo travel guide 2026 brings these different sides of the city together so you can plan them in a logical order.\n\nCairo is not simply a gateway to the rest of Egypt.\n\nThe city and Greater Cairo contain archaeological, religious and cultural sites covering thousands of years of history.\n\nAncient Egypt is represented by Giza, Saqqara, Memphis, Dahshur and the major archaeological museums. Medieval Cairo contains extraordinary Islamic architecture. Old Cairo preserves important Christian heritage. Modern neighborhoods offer restaurants, cafés, hotels, galleries and Nile views.\n\nOne of the biggest reasons to visit now is the Grand Egyptian Museum. Its current standard visit includes the Tutankhamun Galleries, Main Galleries, Grand Hall, Grand Stairs and Khufu’s Boats Museum. The official ticketing platform also publishes extended evening gallery hours on Wednesdays and Saturdays. [Gem Tickets](https://visit-gem.com/)\n\nThis makes Cairo especially valuable for travelers who want more than a quick Pyramids photo stop.\n\n* * *\n\n![Best things to do in Cairo in 2026 including the Giza Pyramids, museums, mosques, markets and Nile experiences](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-17-1024x576.png)Discover Cairo’s top attractions in 2026, from the Giza Pyramids and major museums to historic mosques, markets and Nile experiences.\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Best Things to Do\n\nPlanning more than just Cairo? Explore our [Egypt tours](https://elsahabytours.com/tours/) for private day trips, transfers and custom travel experiences across Luxor, Aswan, Hurghada and other destinations.\n\n## 1\\. Visit the Giza Pyramids and Great Sphinx\n\nNo first visit to Cairo is complete without the Giza Plateau.\n\nThe complex includes:\n\n- Great Pyramid of Khufu\n- Pyramid of Khafre\n- Pyramid of Menkaure\n- Great Sphinx\n- Valley Temple\n- Panoramic viewpoints\n- Smaller pyramids and surrounding archaeological remains\n\nThe Great Pyramid is the last surviving Wonder of the Ancient World and originally stood approximately 146.5 meters high. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/monuments/the-great-pyramid/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n### How Much Time Do You Need at Giza?\n\nAllow around **3–4 hours** for a comfortable visit.\n\nYou may need longer if you want to:\n\n- Enter one of the pyramids\n- Spend time at panoramic viewpoints\n- Visit additional tombs\n- Take professional photos\n- Combine Giza with the Grand Egyptian Museum\n\nDo not plan your visit as a quick one-hour stop. The plateau is large, and moving between different parts of the site takes time.\n\n### 2026 Giza Entrance Information\n\nAt the time of this guide, the official Ministry of Tourism and Antiquities lists the foreign adult Giza Plateau admission at **EGP 700**. Entry inside the Great Pyramid requires a separate ticket, currently listed at **EGP 1,000 for foreign adults**. Prices can change, so always verify them before travel. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/ar/archaeological-sites/giza-plateau?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n**Elsahaby Local Tip:** Visit early, especially during warmer months. Morning temperatures are usually more comfortable, the light is better for many photographs, and you have more flexibility for the rest of your day.\n\nYou can also combine Giza with GEM in one private day:\n\n[Giza Pyramids & Grand Egyptian Museum Tour](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/giza-pyramids-grand-egyptian-museum-tour/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 2\\. Explore the Grand Egyptian Museum\n\nThe Grand Egyptian Museum, commonly called **GEM**, has transformed the Cairo and Giza sightseeing experience.\n\nThe museum is located close to the Giza Pyramids, making the two attractions a logical combination.\n\nThe current official visit includes:\n\n- Tutankhamun Galleries\n- Main Galleries\n- Grand Hall\n- Grand Stairs\n- Khufu’s Boats Museum\n- Exterior gardens\n- Commercial areas\n\nThe official website identifies itself as the **only official website for purchasing GEM tickets**, which is important because travelers may encounter third-party sellers online. [Gem Tickets](https://visit-gem.com/)\n\n### Current GEM Opening Schedule\n\nAccording to the museum’s official ticketing platform:\n\n| Day | Galleries |\n| --- | --- |\n| Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Thursday, Friday | 9:00 AM–6:00 PM |\n| Wednesday and Saturday | 9:00 AM–9:00 PM |\n\nThe wider GEM complex opens earlier, while the last entry is one hour before gallery closing. [Gem Tickets](https://visit-gem.com/)\n\n### How Long Should You Spend at GEM?\n\nFor most first-time visitors:\n\n**3–4 hours** is reasonable.\n\nMuseum lovers may want considerably longer.\n\nTrying to rush through GEM in one hour is not ideal. The scale of the complex and number of galleries make it better approached as one of your major Cairo experiences.\n\n### Giza + GEM in One Day?\n\nYes.\n\nThis is one of the most efficient combinations in Cairo because both are located on the western side of Greater Cairo.\n\nA sensible day can be:\n\n**Morning:** Giza Pyramids\n\n**Lunch:** Giza area\n\n**Afternoon:** Grand Egyptian Museum\n\nAvoid combining GEM with several attractions in Islamic Cairo on the same day unless you have very limited time.\n\n[Official Grand Egyptian Museum ticketing website](https://visit-gem.com/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 3\\. Visit the Egyptian Museum in Tahrir\n\nSome travelers assume that the Grand Egyptian Museum has made the historic Egyptian Museum in Tahrir irrelevant.\n\nThat is not the case.\n\nThe Egyptian Museum remains an important institution with an extensive archaeological collection spanning multiple periods of ancient Egyptian history.\n\nIts central location also makes it easy to combine with Downtown Cairo, the Nile or another nearby experience.\n\nThe Ministry of Tourism and Antiquities currently lists the museum as open **9:00 AM–5:00 PM**, with the ticket office closing at 4:00 PM. Foreign adult admission is currently listed at **EGP 550**. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/ar/museums/egyptian-museum?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n### GEM or Egyptian Museum?\n\nIf you only have time for one major archaeological museum, GEM will usually be the priority for many first-time visitors.\n\nBut travelers with deeper archaeological interest should consider visiting both because their collections and visitor experiences are different.\n\n* * *\n\n## 4\\. Discover the National Museum of Egyptian Civilization\n\nThe **National Museum of Egyptian Civilization — NMEC** in Fustat offers another completely different museum experience.\n\nRather than concentrating only on one archaeological era, its concept presents the development of Egyptian civilization across a broad historical timeline.\n\nOne of its most famous sections is the **Royal Mummies Hall**.\n\nThe official museum currently lists general opening hours from **9:00 AM to 5:00 PM**, with an additional Friday evening opening from **6:00 PM to 9:00 PM**. [NMEC](https://nmec.gov.eg/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nNMEC combines particularly well with:\n\n- Old Cairo\n- Coptic Cairo\n- The Citadel\n- Khan El Khalili\n\nFor travelers wanting a ready-made full-day combination:\n\n[NMEC, Citadel, Khan El Khalili & Old Cairo Tour](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/national-museum-of-egyptian-civilization-citadel-of-saladin-khan-el-khalili-bazaar-old-cairo-tour/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 5\\. Walk Through Historic Cairo\n\nOne of the most rewarding mistakes to avoid is spending your entire Cairo visit only around ancient Egyptian monuments.\n\nHistoric Cairo reveals another extraordinary side of the city.\n\nUNESCO describes Historic Cairo as one of the world’s oldest Islamic cities and notes its major collection of mosques, madrasas, fountains and historic urban architecture. The area became especially important after Cairo’s Fatimid foundation in the 10th century and developed through later Ayyubid, Mamluk and Ottoman periods. [UNESCO World Heritage Centre](https://whc.unesco.org/en/list/89?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nKey places include:\n\n- Al-Muizz Street\n- Bab al-Futuh\n- Bab Zuweila\n- Khan El Khalili\n- Al-Azhar area\n- Historic mosques and madrasas\n- Traditional workshops\n- Old lanes and markets\n\nElsahaby Tours already has a more focused guide to this part of the city:\n\n[Historic Cairo Egypt Guide](https://elsahabytours.com/historic-cairo-egypt-guide/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 6\\. Visit Al-Muizz Street and Khan El Khalili\n\nAl-Muizz Street is one of the best areas in Cairo for travelers who enjoy architecture, photography, history and street atmosphere.\n\nIt is particularly atmospheric in the late afternoon and evening.\n\nNearby **Khan El Khalili** offers:\n\n- Souvenir shops\n- Handmade crafts\n- Lanterns\n- Jewelry\n- Spices\n- Perfumes\n- Traditional cafés\n- Narrow historic lanes\n\nDo not treat Khan El Khalili only as a shopping stop.\n\nThe surrounding streets, buildings, cafés and evening atmosphere are often more memorable than the shopping itself.\n\nA private evening experience is also available:\n\n[Al-Muizz Street & Khan El Khalili Bazaar Tour](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/al-muizz-street-khan-el-khalili-bazaar/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 7\\. Explore the Citadel of Saladin\n\nThe Citadel sits above Cairo and offers both historical significance and wide city views.\n\nConstruction began under Salah al-Din in the 12th century, and the site later became a seat of government for centuries. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/ar/archaeological-sites/cairo-citadel?sa=X)\n\nHighlights include:\n\n- Mosque of Muhammad Ali\n- Al-Nasir Muhammad Mosque\n- Historic fortifications\n- Museums\n- Cairo viewpoints\n\nThe Ministry of Tourism and Antiquities currently lists opening hours from **8:00 AM to 5:00 PM** and foreign adult admission at **EGP 550**. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/ar/archaeological-sites/cairo-citadel?sa=X)\n\nNearby attractions such as Sultan Hassan Mosque and Al-Rifa’i Mosque can make this part of Cairo a strong half-day historical route.\n\n* * *\n\n## 8\\. Discover Coptic Cairo and Old Cairo\n\nOld Cairo offers an important contrast to Giza and Islamic Cairo.\n\nThis area contains some of the most historically important Christian sites in Egypt.\n\nPopular stops include:\n\n- The Hanging Church\n- Church of Saints Sergius and Bacchus\n- Coptic Museum\n- Ben Ezra Synagogue area\n- Fortress of Babylon\n- Nearby Fustat\n\nThe Hanging Church is one of the best-known landmarks in the district, while the Coptic Museum holds an important collection covering the development of Coptic culture and art.\n\nThis area works very well with NMEC because both are in the southern part of central Cairo.\n\n* * *\n\n## 9\\. Take a Day Trip to Saqqara, Memphis and Dahshur\n\nGiza is only one chapter in the history of Egypt’s pyramids.\n\nIf you have a third or fourth day in Cairo, visit:\n\n### Saqqara\n\nHome to the Step Pyramid complex of Djoser, important tombs, the Serapeum and centuries of archaeological history.\n\nThe official site currently lists foreign adult entry to the basic Saqqara area at **EGP 600**, while additional tombs and structures may require separate tickets or an inclusive ticket. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/archaeological-sites/saqqara/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n### Memphis\n\nThe ancient city was one of Egypt’s most important early capitals.\n\n### Dahshur\n\nThis area is especially famous for the Bent Pyramid and Red Pyramid and is essential for understanding the architectural evolution that eventually produced the pyramids at Giza.\n\nFor travelers interested in ancient Egypt, this is one of the most valuable excursions from Cairo.\n\n[Saqqara, Memphis & Dahshur Private Tour](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/half-day-tour-to-saqqara-memphis-dahshur/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 10\\. Enjoy Cairo from the Nile\n\nAfter full sightseeing days, the Nile provides a completely different atmosphere.\n\nDepending on your travel style, you might choose:\n\n- Short Nile boat ride\n- Felucca\n- Dinner cruise\n- Riverside restaurant\n- Zamalek evening\n- Nile-view café\n\nA Nile experience works especially well after museum-heavy or archaeological sightseeing.\n\nFor a more organized evening:\n\n[Cairo Dinner Cruise on the Nile River](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/cairo-dinner-cruise-on-the-nile-river/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## 11\\. Explore Cairo Beyond the Famous Attractions\n\nTravelers with four or five days can go deeper.\n\nInteresting additions include:\n\n### Sultan Hassan Mosque\n\nOne of Cairo’s most impressive examples of Mamluk architecture.\n\n### Al-Rifa’i Mosque\n\nLocated beside Sultan Hassan and visually forming one of Cairo’s most impressive architectural combinations.\n\n### Nilometer\n\nAn important reminder of the historical relationship between Egypt and the Nile.\n\n### Manial Palace\n\nA fascinating palace complex with distinctive architecture and decorative arts.\n\n### Cave Church of Saint Simon\n\nLocated in Mokattam and carved dramatically into the mountain.\n\n### Zamalek\n\nA calmer Nile island neighborhood with cafés, restaurants, galleries and hotels.\n\n### Downtown Cairo\n\nUseful for architecture, city life, cafés and exploring Cairo beyond its archaeological monuments.\n\nElsahaby Tours also offers a route focusing on some less obvious sites:\n\n[Cairo Hidden Gems Tour](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/sultan-hassan-mosque-nilometer-and-mohamed-ali-palace/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n![How many days to spend in Cairo with suggested 2-day, 3-day and 4-day itineraries](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-20-1024x576.png)Plan the ideal Cairo stay with practical 2-day, 3-day and 4-day itinerary options for first-time visitors.\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: How Many Days Do You Need?\n\n## One Day\n\nPossible, but very rushed.\n\nChoose:\n\n**Giza Pyramids + Grand Egyptian Museum**\n\nDo not attempt to see all of Cairo.\n\n## Two Days\n\nGood minimum for first-time visitors.\n\n### Day 1\n\nGiza Pyramids + GEM\n\n### Day 2\n\nCitadel + Historic Cairo + Khan El Khalili\n\nYou will still miss several major sites.\n\n## Three Days\n\nThis is our recommended minimum for a balanced trip.\n\n### Day 1 – Ancient Giza\n\nGiza Pyramids\n\nGreat Sphinx\n\nGrand Egyptian Museum\n\n### Day 2 – Historic Cairo\n\nCitadel\n\nMosque of Muhammad Ali\n\nOld Cairo\n\nNMEC\n\nKhan El Khalili\n\n### Day 3 – Pyramid Evolution\n\nSaqqara\n\nMemphis\n\nDahshur\n\nRelaxing Nile evening\n\n## Four Days\n\nFour days gives you much more flexibility.\n\nAdd:\n\n- Egyptian Museum in Tahrir\n- Al-Muizz Street\n- More time in Old Cairo\n- Zamalek\n- Cairo food experiences\n- Hidden historical sites\n\nFor most first-time travelers, **3–4 days is the ideal Cairo itinerary**.\n\n* * *\n\nThe following Cairo travel guide 2026 itinerary is designed to reduce unnecessary travel time while covering the city’s major highlights.\n\n## Suggested 4-Day Cairo Itinerary 2026\n\n| Day | Morning | Afternoon | Evening |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Day 1 | Giza Pyramids & Sphinx | Grand Egyptian Museum | Relax |\n| Day 2 | Saqqara | Memphis & Dahshur | Nile dinner or rest |\n| Day 3 | NMEC & Old Cairo | Citadel | Khan El Khalili |\n| Day 4 | Egyptian Museum / Downtown | Al-Muizz or Zamalek | Nile or local dining |\n\nThis itinerary avoids unnecessary back-and-forth across the city.\n\nThat matters because Cairo traffic can significantly affect travel times.\n\n* * *\n\n![Where to stay in Cairo including Downtown, Zamalek, Giza and New Cairo](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-27-1024x576.png)Compare the best areas to stay in Cairo, including Downtown, Zamalek, Giza and New Cairo, based on your itinerary and travel style.\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Where to Stay in Cairo\n\nOne of the most important decisions in any Cairo travel guide 2026 plan is choosing the right neighborhood for your itinerary.\n\nYour hotel location can change your entire Cairo experience.\n\n## Giza\n\n### Best For\n\nPyramids, GEM and first-time visitors prioritizing ancient Egypt.\n\n### Advantages\n\nClose to Giza attractions and many pyramid-view hotels.\n\n### Disadvantages\n\nFarther from Downtown, Historic Cairo and some nightlife areas.\n\n### Elsahaby Tip\n\nIf a hotel advertises a “Pyramid View,” check the exact room category. Some rooms may have only partial or distant views.\n\n* * *\n\n## Zamalek\n\n### Best For\n\nCouples, restaurants, cafés and longer stays.\n\nZamalek sits on Gezira Island and generally offers a more relaxed atmosphere than some busier parts of central Cairo.\n\nIt can be a good compromise for travelers who want access to central Cairo while enjoying a calmer base.\n\n* * *\n\n## Downtown Cairo\n\n### Best For\n\nTravelers who enjoy urban exploration and central location.\n\nAdvantages include access to:\n\n- Egyptian Museum\n- Talaat Harb area\n- Restaurants\n- Historic buildings\n- Nile\n\nTraffic and street noise vary considerably by hotel.\n\n* * *\n\n## Garden City\n\n### Best For\n\nTravelers wanting centrality with a more residential atmosphere.\n\nIt is well positioned for the Nile, Downtown and several major hotels.\n\n* * *\n\n## Heliopolis\n\n### Best For\n\nShort stays, late arrivals and travelers prioritizing airport access.\n\nIt is convenient for Cairo International Airport but farther from Giza.\n\n* * *\n\n## New Cairo\n\n### Best For\n\nModern hotels, business travelers, newer districts and airport-side stays.\n\nIt is not the best location if most of your itinerary is Giza and Historic Cairo.\n\n* * *\n\n## Giza vs Cairo: Where Should You Stay?\n\nThis is one of the most common planning questions.\n\n### Stay in Giza if:\n\n- Pyramids are your priority.\n- You want pyramid views.\n- GEM is a major focus.\n- You only have one or two nights.\n\n### Stay in central Cairo if:\n\n- You have 3–5 nights.\n- You want museums, Islamic Cairo and restaurants.\n- You prefer a more central base.\n\n### Stay in Zamalek if:\n\n- You want a balance of location and atmosphere.\n- Restaurants and evenings matter.\n- You do not need to wake up facing the Pyramids.\n\nThere is no universally “best” neighborhood.\n\nThe best location is the one that matches your itinerary.\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: How to Get Around Cairo\n\nCairo is enormous.\n\nDo not assume you can walk between major attractions just because they look close on an online map.\n\n## Private Car and Driver\n\nFor sightseeing, this is one of the easiest options.\n\nBenefits include:\n\n- Hotel pickup\n- Air conditioning\n- Door-to-door travel\n- Luggage flexibility\n- Less time negotiating transportation\n- Easier multi-stop itineraries\n\nPrivate transportation is particularly useful for Giza, Saqqara and Dahshur.\n\n* * *\n\n## Ride-Hailing Apps\n\nRide-hailing services can be convenient for simple point-to-point journeys.\n\nAllow additional time during:\n\n- Morning commuting periods\n- Late afternoon\n- Thursday evenings\n- Major events\n- Central Cairo congestion\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo Metro\n\nThe metro is inexpensive and useful for some central routes.\n\nThe Cairo Metro’s official information currently states that regular operating hours are approximately **5:00 AM to midnight**, with different schedules possible during Ramadan. [Cairo Metro](https://cairometro.gov.eg/en/passengers/1?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nHowever, it is not the easiest transportation method for every tourist attraction.\n\nYou will still need other transportation for places such as the Pyramids and Saqqara.\n\n[Official Cairo Metro website](https://www.cairometro.gov.eg/en?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Walking\n\nWalking is excellent inside specific districts:\n\n- Al-Muizz\n- Khan El Khalili\n- Downtown sections\n- Old Cairo\n- Zamalek\n\nBut walking between distant Cairo attractions is generally unrealistic.\n\nTraffic crossings can also feel unfamiliar for first-time visitors.\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo Airport Transportation\n\nCairo International Airport is northeast of central Cairo.\n\nTravel time depends heavily on:\n\n- Terminal\n- Hotel location\n- Traffic\n- Time of day\n\nA transfer to Giza may take considerably longer than one to Heliopolis.\n\nThe airport’s official website lists taxis, limousine services, buses and car rental among the available transport options. [Cairo Airport](https://www.cairo-airport.com/en-us/Services/Passenger-Guide/Move-From-To-Airport?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nFor travelers arriving after a long international flight, pre-arranged transportation is often the simplest option.\n\nElsahaby Tours offers private airport pickup with meet-and-greet:\n\n[Cairo Airport Transfer with Meet & Greet](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/cairo-airport-transfer-service-with-meet-greet/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nYou can also read:\n\n[Cairo Airport to Giza Transport Guide](https://elsahabytours.com/cairo-airport-to-giza-best-transport-options-costs-safety-tips/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n![Cairo travel costs and daily budget for hotels, food, transportation and sightseeing in 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-25-1024x576.png)Estimate your Cairo travel budget for hotels, meals, transport, entrance tickets and sightseeing experiences.\n\n## Cairo Travel Costs in 2026\n\nThis Cairo travel guide 2026 uses practical budget ranges rather than fixed prices because travel costs vary by season and travel style.\n\nCairo can accommodate many different budgets.\n\nYour total cost depends less on food and more on choices such as:\n\n- Hotel standard\n- Entrance tickets\n- Private tours\n- Guides\n- Transportation\n- Pyramid entry tickets\n- Restaurants\n- Shopping\n\n## Approximate Daily Planning Budget\n\n| Travel Style | Suggested Daily Budget\\* |\n| --- | --- |\n| Budget traveler | $35–60 |\n| Comfortable mid-range | $70–150 |\n| Private / premium travel | $150–300+ |\n\n\\*These are planning estimates, not fixed prices. International flights and major luxury accommodation can significantly increase the total.\n\nMuseum and archaeological ticket prices can also add up quickly if you visit several sites.\n\nFor a much more detailed Egypt-wide breakdown:\n\n[Egypt Travel Costs 2026 Guide](https://elsahabytours.com/egypt-travel-costs/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Major Cairo Attraction Costs: 2026 Snapshot\n\nThese official prices were checked while preparing this guide and may change.\n\n| Attraction | Foreign Adult Admission |\n| --- | --- |\n| Giza Plateau | EGP 700 |\n| Great Pyramid interior | EGP 1,000 + plateau admission |\n| Egyptian Museum | EGP 550 |\n| NMEC | EGP 550 |\n| Cairo Citadel | EGP 550 |\n| Saqqara basic area | EGP 600 |\n\nOfficial Ministry pages currently list these prices for Giza, the Great Pyramid, Egyptian Museum, NMEC, Citadel and Saqqara. Always verify prices before your visit because Egypt’s archaeological site fees may be updated. [Egyptian Monuments](https://egymonuments.gov.eg/ar/archaeological-sites/giza-plateau?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n**Important:** Extras such as pyramid interiors, tombs and premium tickets may require separate admission.\n\n* * *\n\n![Unknown 19](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-19-1024x576.png)\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Best Time to Visit Cairo\n\nCairo can be visited year-round.\n\nHowever, weather affects how comfortable long outdoor sightseeing days feel.\n\n## October to April\n\nGenerally the most comfortable period for:\n\n- Pyramids\n- Saqqara\n- Dahshur\n- Walking in Historic Cairo\n- Long outdoor tours\n\nYour existing Egypt weather guide also recommends the cooler October-to-April period for Cairo and Giza sightseeing. [Elsahaby Tours](https://elsahabytours.com/best-time-to-visit-egypt/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n[Best Time to Visit Egypt 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/best-time-to-visit-egypt/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## May to September\n\nCairo remains open and active.\n\nThe key is smarter scheduling.\n\nDuring warmer months:\n\n- Start Giza early.\n- Use museums during hotter hours.\n- Carry water.\n- Wear a hat.\n- Use sunscreen.\n- Avoid packing too much outdoor sightseeing into midday.\n- Build rest time into the itinerary.\n\n* * *\n\n## What to Eat in Cairo\n\nCairo is an excellent place to experience Egyptian food.\n\nPopular dishes include:\n\n### Koshari\n\nRice, pasta, lentils, chickpeas, tomato sauce and crispy onions.\n\n### Ful Medames\n\nSlow-cooked fava beans, often served at breakfast.\n\n### Taameya\n\nEgyptian-style falafel traditionally prepared with fava beans.\n\n### Hawawshi\n\nSeasoned minced meat baked inside bread.\n\n### Kofta and Kebab\n\nCommon grilled-meat choices.\n\n### Molokhia\n\nA traditional green soup-like dish served with rice or bread and often meat.\n\n### Egyptian Desserts\n\nTry Om Ali, basbousa, konafa and rice pudding.\n\nFor the full food guide:\n\n[Egyptian Food Guide 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/egyptian-food-guide/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Is Cairo Safe for Tourists in 2026?\n\nMillions of visitors travel through Cairo, but it is still a major world city and sensible precautions matter.\n\nCommon tourist concerns are often practical rather than dramatic:\n\n- Heavy traffic\n- Persistent sellers\n- Unofficial services\n- Overcharging\n- Crowded areas\n- Protecting phones and belongings\n\n![Cairo safety tips and practical travel advice for tourists visiting Egypt](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-21-1024x576.png)Practical safety tips for visiting Cairo, including transport, local etiquette, personal belongings and smooth travel planning.\n\n## Practical Cairo Safety Tips\n\nUse trusted transportation.\n\nConfirm prices before accepting unofficial services.\n\nKeep unnecessary valuables at the hotel.\n\nDo not allow strangers to take control of your itinerary or lead you into shops you did not request.\n\nKeep your hotel details available offline.\n\nAllow extra travel time rather than rushing.\n\nUse normal caution with bags and phones in crowded markets.\n\nAvoid demonstrations or political gatherings.\n\nFor a more complete country-wide guide:\n\n[Is Egypt Safe to Travel in 2026?](https://elsahabytours.com/is-egypt-safe-to-travel-in-2026-essential-safety-tips-for-tourists/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n![Common Cairo travel mistakes including traffic, hotel location, transportation, distances and ticket planning](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-29-1024x576.png)Avoid common Cairo travel mistakes such as underestimating distances, ignoring traffic, choosing the wrong hotel location and failing to check tickets or opening hours.\n\n## Common Cairo Travel Mistakes\n\n## Trying to See Everything in One Day\n\nCairo looks manageable on a map but traffic and attraction size make over-packed itineraries exhausting.\n\n## Staying in the Wrong Area\n\nA beautiful hotel is not always conveniently located for your sightseeing plan.\n\n## Scheduling Giza at Midday in Summer\n\nEarly morning is usually far more comfortable.\n\n## Combining Giza, Saqqara, Old Cairo and Khan El Khalili in One Day\n\nTechnically possible with very rushed timing. Not recommended.\n\n## Assuming All Pyramid Tickets Are Included\n\nGeneral plateau admission does not automatically include every pyramid interior or tomb.\n\n## Buying GEM Tickets from Random Websites\n\nThe museum states that its official ticketing platform is the only official online source for GEM tickets. [Gem Tickets](https://visit-gem.com/)\n\n## Using Unplanned Shopping Stops\n\nSome tours can become dominated by shops.\n\nAsk exactly what is included before starting.\n\nElsahaby Tours follows a **no forced shopping** approach on private itineraries.\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo for Families\n\nCairo can work very well for families when the days are not overloaded.\n\nGood family experiences include:\n\n- Giza Pyramids\n- GEM\n- NMEC\n- Nile boat rides\n- Saqqara\n- Short Khan El Khalili visit\n\nAllow rest periods and avoid multiple large museums in one day with younger children.\n\nA private vehicle can make family logistics easier, especially with strollers, bags and hot weather.\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo for Couples\n\nCouples can combine sightseeing with slower experiences such as:\n\n- Pyramid-view sunset\n- Nile dinner\n- Zamalek restaurants\n- Evening Al-Muizz walk\n- Private sightseeing\n- Luxury hotels\n- Photography experiences\n\nFor couples planning a wider Egypt trip:\n\n[Egypt Honeymoon Guide 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/egypt-honeymoon-guide/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo for Solo Travelers\n\nSolo travelers can explore Cairo successfully, but a little preparation improves the experience.\n\nKeep transportation apps ready.\n\nDownload maps.\n\nUse licensed guides when you want historical explanation.\n\nDo not feel pressured into shopping.\n\nKeep small amounts of cash accessible.\n\nFor complicated day trips such as Saqqara and Dahshur, organized transportation can save substantial time.\n\n* * *\n\n## Best Day Trips from Cairo\n\n## Alexandria\n\nA full-day trip can include Mediterranean scenery and major historical sites.\n\n[Alexandria Day Tour from Cairo](https://elsahabytours.com/trip/day-tour-to-alexandria-from-cairo/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n## Fayoum\n\nA good choice for desert landscapes, lakes and nature.\n\n## White Desert\n\nUsually better as an overnight experience rather than a short day trip.\n\n## Siwa Oasis\n\nSiwa is not a realistic Cairo day trip.\n\nPlan several days.\n\n## Saqqara, Memphis and Dahshur\n\nThe best archaeological extension from Cairo and easily manageable in a full sightseeing day.\n\n* * *\n\n## Traveling from Cairo to Luxor\n\nAfter Cairo, Luxor is one of the most logical next stops.\n\nMain options include:\n\n- Domestic flight\n- Train\n- Sleeper train\n- Bus\n- Private transfer\n\nFlights are fastest.\n\nTrains appeal to travelers wanting an overland journey.\n\nPrivate transfers give maximum flexibility but involve a long drive.\n\nFor a detailed comparison:\n\n[How to Travel from Cairo to Luxor](https://elsahabytours.com/cairo-to-luxor/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo to Aswan\n\nMost travelers reach Aswan from Cairo by:\n\n- Domestic flight\n- Overnight train\n- Sleeper train\n- Combination via Luxor\n\nIf your itinerary includes Luxor as well, a common route is:\n\n**Cairo → Luxor → Nile Cruise or Road → Aswan**\n\nrather than jumping back and forth between cities.\n\n* * *\n\n## Cairo to Hurghada\n\nHurghada works well after several culture-heavy days in Cairo.\n\nOptions include:\n\n- Domestic flight\n- Bus\n- Private transfer\n\nThis creates a good combination of:\n\n**Ancient Egypt + Museums + Red Sea relaxation**\n\n* * *\n\n## Visa and Entry Planning\n\nVisa requirements depend on nationality.\n\nEgypt has an official e-Visa portal for eligible travelers.\n\nThe official portal states that applicants should submit e-Visa applications at least **seven days before departure**. [Visa2Egypt](https://visa2egypt.gov.eg/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n[Official Egypt e-Visa Portal](https://visa2egypt.gov.eg/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\nFor a full explanation:\n\n[Egypt Visa Requirements 2026](https://elsahabytours.com/egypt-visa-requirements/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## Money, SIM Cards and Internet\n\nDo not rely on one payment method.\n\nCarry:\n\n- Bank card\n- Small amount of Egyptian cash\n- Backup payment method\n\nATMs are widely available in Cairo.\n\nA local SIM or eSIM makes ride-hailing, maps and WhatsApp much easier.\n\nUseful guides:\n\n[Money in Egypt: Cash, Cards & ATMs](https://elsahabytours.com/money-in-egypt-cash-cards-atms/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n[Egypt SIM Card & eSIM Guide](https://elsahabytours.com/egypt-sim-card-esim-internet-guide/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n## What to Pack for Cairo\n\nBring lightweight, breathable clothing for warm periods.\n\nComfortable walking shoes are essential.\n\nAlso consider:\n\n- Sunglasses\n- Hat\n- Sunscreen\n- Refillable water bottle\n- Portable phone charger\n- Light layer for cooler winter evenings\n- Modest clothing for religious sites\n- Small day bag\n- Copies of important documents\n\nFor a complete Egypt-wide list:\n\n[Egypt Packing List](https://elsahabytours.com/egypt-packing-list/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\n![Frequently asked questions about Cairo travel in 2026 covering safety, costs, transport, attractions and where to stay](https://elsahabytours.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Unknown-32-1024x576.png)Answers to common questions about visiting Cairo in 2026, including safety, transport, costs, attractions, accommodation and trip planning.\n\n## Cairo Travel Guide 2026: Frequently Asked Questions\n\n## Is Cairo worth visiting in 2026?\n\nAbsolutely. Cairo combines the Giza Pyramids, Grand Egyptian Museum, major archaeological museums, Islamic architecture, Christian heritage, markets, Nile experiences and modern city life in one destination.\n\n## How many days should I spend in Cairo?\n\nThree to four days is ideal for most first-time visitors. Two days is possible but requires prioritizing attractions.\n\n## Can I see Cairo in one day?\n\nYou can see one major part of Cairo, but not the whole city. If you have one day, focus on Giza Pyramids and GEM.\n\n## Should I stay in Cairo or Giza?\n\nStay in Giza if the Pyramids and GEM are your priority. Choose central Cairo or Zamalek for a more balanced multi-day city experience.\n\n## Is the Grand Egyptian Museum near the Pyramids?\n\nYes. GEM and the Giza Plateau are located close enough to combine comfortably in one sightseeing day.\n\n## Do I still need to visit the Egyptian Museum after GEM?\n\nNot necessarily for a short trip, but serious history enthusiasts may enjoy both because the institutions offer different collections and experiences.\n\n## What is the best month to visit Cairo?\n\nNovember through March usually offer especially comfortable temperatures, although October and April are also excellent choices.\n\n## Is Cairo expensive?\n\nIt can be relatively affordable compared with many international capitals, but entrance tickets, private guides, premium hotels and private transportation increase the total budget.\n\n## Can tourists use the Cairo Metro?\n\nYes. The metro can be useful for some central journeys, although it does not replace road transportation for all major attractions.\n\n## Is Uber or ride-hailing available in Cairo?\n\nRide-hailing apps are commonly used for point-to-point transportation, but travel times still depend heavily on traffic.\n\n## Is Cairo walkable?\n\nIndividual neighborhoods are walkable, but Cairo as a whole is far too large to explore entirely on foot.\n\n## Is Khan El Khalili worth visiting?\n\nYes, particularly in the late afternoon or evening. Visit for atmosphere and historic surroundings, not only shopping.\n\n## Is Saqqara worth visiting if I already saw Giza?\n\nYes. Saqqara and Dahshur explain how pyramid architecture developed before Giza and offer a very different archaeological experience.\n\n## Do I need a guide in Cairo?\n\nNot always, but a qualified guide adds significant value at archaeological and historic sites, especially for travelers who want more than basic sightseeing.\n\n## Can I customize a Cairo private tour?\n\nYes. Private itineraries can be organized around your available time, preferred attractions, hotel location and travel pace.\n\n* * *\n\n## Elsahaby Team’s Ideal First-Time Cairo Plan\n\nFor most first-time visitors, we recommend **three full sightseeing days**.\n\n**Day 1:**\n\nGiza Pyramids → Great Sphinx → Grand Egyptian Museum\n\n**Day 2:**\n\nNMEC → Old Cairo → Citadel → Khan El Khalili\n\n**Day 3:**\n\nSaqqara → Memphis → Dahshur → Nile evening\n\nIf you have a fourth day, add the Egyptian Museum, Downtown, Al-Muizz Street, Zamalek or Cairo’s lesser-known historical sites.\n\nThis plan provides a much better understanding of Cairo than trying to squeeze every attraction into two rushed days.\n\n* * *\n\n## Experience Cairo with Elsahaby Tours\n\nCairo can feel complicated when you try to organize every museum, ticket, district and transfer independently.\n\nAt **Elsahaby Tours**, we have been helping travelers explore Egypt **since 1993**.\n\nOur approach is simple: your itinerary should fit you, not force you into a fixed program.\n\nWe can assist with:\n\n- Private Cairo transportation\n- Cairo International Airport transfers\n- Giza Pyramids tours\n- Grand Egyptian Museum visits\n- Saqqara, Memphis and Dahshur\n- Historic Cairo\n- Old Cairo\n- NMEC\n- Khan El Khalili\n- Alexandria day trips\n- Egyptologist guides\n- Multi-day Egypt itineraries\n- Luxor, Aswan and Red Sea connections\n\nWe believe in making friends and family before business. You can ask for help with your itinerary before booking, compare options and decide what works best for you.\n\nThere is **no pressure to book and no forced shopping**.\n\n[Explore all Egypt tours](https://elsahabytours.com/tours/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n[Contact Elsahaby Tours](https://elsahabytours.com/contact-us/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n* * *\n\nA well-planned Cairo travel guide 2026 should help you see more without turning every day into a rushed checklist.\n\n## Final Thoughts: Is Cairo Worth It?\n\nCairo is intense, enormous, historic and unforgettable.\n\nIt is not a destination that should be judged by one traffic jam, one busy road or one rushed day tour.\n\nPlan it correctly and you can move through thousands of years of history in only a few days: from early pyramid building at Saqqara and Dahshur to the monumental Pyramids of Giza, from ancient royal collections to Christian Fustat, medieval Islamic Cairo and the modern banks of the Nile.\n\nThe best Cairo trip is not the one with the greatest number of stops.\n\nIt is the one that gives each part of the city enough time to make sense.\n\nFor most travelers, three or four well-planned days provide the right balance of ancient history, museums, local culture and modern Cairo.\n\nAnd when your Cairo journey ends, Egypt still has Luxor, Aswan, Abu Simbel, the Nile, the Western Desert and the Red Sea waiting for you.\n\n* * *\n\n## Useful Links\n\n### Elsahaby Tours\n\n[Cairo Destination & Tours](https://elsahabytours.com/destinations/cairo/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)\n\n[Historic Cairo Egypt         "url": "https://wortheverymile.com/en/africa/egypt/travel-guide-for-cairo/",
        "title": "Cairo, Egypt: The ultimate first-timer's Travel Guide | Worth Every Mile",
        "description": "# Cairo, Egypt: The ultimate first-timer’s Travel Guide\n- [Africa](https://wortheverymile.com/en/category/africa/), [Egypt](https://wortheverymile.com/en/category/africa/egypt/)\n\n#### Table of Contents\n|  |  |\n|-|-|\n| **Country** | Egypt |\n| **City population** | ~9.8 million (Greater Cairo: ~23 million) |\n| **Nickname** | “City of a Thousand Minarets” / “Mother of the World” |\n| **Time zone** | EET — UTC+2 (no daylight saving) |\n| **Best time to visit** | October–April |\n| **Airport** | Cairo International Airport (CAI) — largest in Egypt |\n| **Metro** | 3 lines — the oldest metro in Africa (since 1987) |\n| **Visa** | Required — 👉 **[iVisa.com](https://www.ivisa.com/?utm_source=wortheverymile)** |\n| **Tours & activities** | 👉 [**GetYourGuide — Cairo**](https://www.getyourguide.com/cairo-l92/?partner_id=85CKKC8&utm_medium=affiliate&placement=button-cta&cmp=cairo) |\n| **Accommodation** | 👉 [**Booking.com — Cairo**](https://www.booking.com/searchresults.el.html?ss=Cairo&label=gen173nr-10CAEoggI46AdIM1gEaFyIAQGYATO4ARfIAQzYAQPoAQH4AQGIAgGoAgG4Ava_qM4GwAIB0gIkNGE5N2RjYjYtYTMyMS00ZTJmLWFlNjEtZTExNTllZGRiYjU02AIB4AIB&sid=ba81ec935d2f2e2ac1b72dc29a634f78&aid=304142&lang=el&sb=1&src_elem=sb&src=index&dest_id=-290692&dest_type=city&ac_position=0&ac_click_type=b&ac_langcode=en&ac_suggestion_list_length=5&search_selected=true&search_pageview_id=27a35ce488080253&ac_meta=GhAyN2EzNWNlNDg4MDgwMjUzIAAoATICZW46BUNhaXJv&group_adults=2&no_rooms=1&group_children=0) |\n\n## Transportation\n##### **From the airport**\n**VIP Cars:** At the exits, you will find 7-seat minivans that will take you to your accommodation for a predetermined price.\n\n**Taxi:** You can get black-and-white taxis at a fixed fare of 50-80 EGP, or newer white taxis equipped with a taximeter. However, certain drivers might set a starting price above 100 EGP because they know some tourists negotiate.\n\nCheck prices on **[Welcome Pickups](https://www.welcomepickups.com/?tap_a=26297-438366&tap_s=3089898-02f59c)**\n\n**Uber:** The well-known app will free you from haggling; you will already know the price of the ride and feel more secure, as you can track your journey’s route.\n\n**Bus:** Bus 356 connects Terminal 1 (international flights) to the city center. However, it is not preferred as the timetables are not respected, and there are delays.\n\nIt is a 45-minute distance depending on the traffic. To secure your journey from the airport, we suggest booking a hotel that includes airport transportation as part of its services. In any other occasion, the experience of finding and negotiating a price with one of the many drivers would feel chaotic and frustrating.\n\n##### **Transportation within the city**\n**Underground Metro:** 3 lines covering the city. The oldest metro in Africa (opened 1987), ranking among the 15 busiest globally. Fares: EGP 5–10 depending on distance (~$0.30). Cheap and efficient for the main city areas.\n\nTo get to the Pyramids from downtown: Take Metro Line 2 to Giza station (6 stops from Tahrir, ~EGP 6), then take a shared microbus or taxi for the final stretch.\n\n**Tok Tok:** You will have to negotiate and price as there is no meter on the tricycle, but you will experience its vibrant adventure.\n\n**Taxi:** We do not recommend using a taxi within the city. You will find it challenging to communicate with the drivers, as most speak little to no English. The times we took the taxi in Cairo, we showed the address to the driver, and he detoured, forcing the price of the meter to go up (whereas some were not even using the meter), and at the end, they asked for double the meter price, providing us with less change at the end.\n\nUber is the safest and most transparent taxi option in Cairo, with prices set in advance and routes tracked. Avoid street taxis, which often overcharge tourists. A 30-minute journey from Downtown to Khan el-Khalili costs approximately EGP 100–150 by Uber.\n\n**VIP Transport:** Private transport with a driver or tourist guide, depending on the package. Many hotels offer organized tours with a driver or tourist guide. However, some hotels act as intermediaries with local drivers and take a commission. For this reason, make sure to research the prices of the tours you desire.\n\n## Best things to do in Cairo\n##### **Giza Necropolis**\nThe pyramids are among the most famous tourist sights in the world. Thousands of tourists flock to Egypt every year, specifically to this region. Even though it is widely cited as a tourist site in Cairo, it is in the Giza region, which is very close.\n\nPayment at the Giza complex is by card only; cash is no longer accepted. Buy tickets in advance online at egymonuments.com or book a guided tour.\n\n**Current entry fees (foreigners):**\n\n##### **Grand Egyptian Museum (GEM) — The World’s Largest Museum**\n- **Location:** Giza, next to the Ring Road — ~15 minutes from downtown Cairo\n- **Tickets:** Foreign adults EGP 1,450 (~$29); students/children EGP 730. Must be booked online only at **[visit-gem.com](https://visit-gem.com/)**\n- **Hours:** 8:30 am–10:00 pm daily\n\n##### **Excursion to Alexandria**\nThe city of **[Alexandria](https://wortheverymile.com/en/africa/egypt/travel-guide-for-alexandria/)** is less than 3 hours away. Alexandria is the second-largest city and the main port of Egypt.\n\nBus routes from Cairo to Alexandria can be found on **[Bookaway](https://www.bookaway.com/?offer_id=34&aff_id=2419)**\n\n## Useful Sites & Apps\n##### **Welcome Pickups**\nOne of the best, most trustworthy, and most convenient 5-star airport taxi companies for your airport transfer based on user reviews across various platforms such as Google, Tripadvisor, and Trustpilot. Local English-speaking drivers, flat, pre-paid fee, flight monitoring for delays, 24/7 Customer support + free cancellation.\n\n##### **Air Bnb**\nWith more than 1 million properties worldwide, the options never end, ideal for low-budget opportunities even in expensive destinations.\n\n##### **Omio.com**\nWith a comprehensive network of trains, buses, and flights across Europe and beyond, Omio allows you to compare and choose the best travel options for your needs. Check available routes on **[Omio.com](http://omio.sjv.io/PLkDY)**.",
